Meeting records Transcript


Summary
Roadmap updates included earlier X Flash availability, 2.5 Flash deprecation extension, with new multimodal embeddings and 3.1 Pro bash native execution discussed.


Model Roadmap and Availability
Model availability was updated, with X Flash launching earlier at the end of April and 2.5 Flash deprecation moved to October to ensure a 5-to-6 month GA overlap. The new multimodal Gemini embeddings model was introduced, showing strong performance in image search and making it suitable for low latency search applications.


New Product and API Capabilities
The 3.1 Pro model is a bash native execution model, providing a significant boost for long-range task planning and coding use cases. The Gemini 2.5 Live API is launching with parity fixes for AI studio, including asynchronous non-blocking deferred function calling responses for long-running jobs.


Agent and Deep Research Launches
The public preview of the Deep Research Agent with the Interactions API is launching, allowing users to upload local or Google Drive files for deep research planning and execution. The Tool Registry and hybrid orchestration are being introduced to allow low-code/no-code agents to access tools via the registry, simplifying complex agent calling hierarchies.


Next steps
                           * [ ] [Vamsi Ramakrishnan] Share Demo Video: Provide the live avatar product demo video to someone in Hayman (au-syd-pir). Complete task in the next couple days.
                           * [ ] [Vamsi Ramakrishnan] Share Live Demo: Get access to the DBS live avatar demo. Share access with someone in Hayman (au-syd-pir).
                           * [ ] [Vamsi Ramakrishnan] Send Whitepaper: Push the TTSDS white paper document to someone in Hayman (au-syd-pir).


Details
                           * Model Roadmap and Deprecation Updates: Vamsi Ramakrishnan provided updates on model availability, noting that X Flash will go live earlier, at the end of April, and 3.x Flash light is also scheduled for May. The deprecation dates for 2.5 flash have been moved to October to ensure a 5-to-6 month overlap between two generally available (GA) models, allowing customers enough time to migrate. They clarified that sunsetting for single-zone TPU regions, such as Australia, will not happen until a replacement model is available in the region with at least one quarter of GA overlap.
                           * Gemini Embeddings for Multimodal Search: Vamsi Ramakrishnan introduced the new multimodal Gemini embeddings model, which has shown strong performance in image search, outperforming previous multimodal text embedding models. This new model is ranked highly on the MTB leaderboard, making it suitable for low latency search applications and enabling teams to revive vector search conversations with interested customers. Customers looking for better controls relative to Vertex search may find this approach beneficial.
                           * 3.1 Pro Model and Bash Native Execution: The 3.1 Pro model is a bash native execution model, meaning both 3.1 Pro and 3 Flash prefer using bash tools for handling large sets of operations and file manipulation. This capability provides a significant boost for long-range task planning and execution, especially for coding use cases, and was a factor in Atlassian's decision to shift from Sonnet to 3 Flash.
                           * Launch of Live Avatar Product Private Preview: A new "live avatar product" is being launched, with the private preview starting on April 9th, allowing customers to have video-based sessions with an avatar. The avatar demo is based on their native audio model and is closer to Tavis technology, with the video avatar powered by the same model as Genie 3, rendering live expressions and reacting to audio tonalities. Vamsi Ramakrishnan plans to share a live avatar demo video in the next couple of days.
                           * Gemini 2.5 Live API Improvements: The Gemini 2.5 Live API is launching with several parity fixes for AI studio, including deferred function calling response. This introduces asynchronous non-blocking function calling, which means the conversation can continue without pausing while waiting for a function response, and context can be injected back into the system at turn boundaries to avoid interruptions. This capability supports long-running jobs, allowing the conversation to flow naturally even if a background process takes several minutes.
                           * Public Preview of Deep Research Agent with Interactions API: The public preview of the Deep Research Agent with the Interactions API is being launched, allowing users to upload local or Google Drive files for deep research planning and execution, similar to the existing Gemini deep research functionality. This agent supports background execution, multimodal inputs, and an interaction object for a persistent, iterable display on the user experience. MCP support is being developed to allow the agent to trigger multiple downstream tool calls beyond Google searches, with the launch planned around Cloud Next.
                           * Deep Research Agent Customer Use Cases: The Deep Research Agent is suitable for various business applications, such as real estate websites generating deep research reports across multiple locations, which can then be wired to a coding agent to generate personalized, interactive websites. Other opportunities include high-consideration use cases like recruiters doing research on candidate profiles or HR customers analyzing aggregated employee engagement statistics.
                           * Voice Cloning and Audio Conditioned Video for 3.1: Vamsi Ramakrishnan confirmed that audio-conditioned video is launching for V3.1, which includes two main capabilities: voice cloning by uploading an audio file and using text to generate dialogue in a similar voice, and audio-conditioned video generation for lip syncing and dubbing. These features are designed to improve consistency in video generation and are planned to launch around Cloud Next.
                           * Gemini 3 Flash TTS Voice Replication Quality: Vamsi Ramakrishnan discussed the launch of the 3 Flash TTS model, which aims to provide low latency voice replication for live applications and improve on previous voice cloning quality issues. Early access has started, and internal evaluations indicate that the new system beats Lemon Labs, particularly on vernacular languages and accents. There will be three versions—Flash, Pro, and HD—with 3 Flash TTS launching in public preview by the end of the week, and voice replication launching post-Next, around May 11th.
                           * Updates to Video Generation Models (V3): Updates to the V3 model include V3 full fine-tune (for large-scale customers) and V3 Lora fine-tune (for smaller studios), as well as an upsampler for 4K video generation and V3.1R2V for generating video based on provided ingredients and style. They noted that audio-conditioned video generation is also part of this combination of V3 capabilities.
                           * Agent Capabilities and Tool Registry: New features for agents include the launch of the tool registry, which will be announced at Cloud Next and will allow low-code/no-code agents to access tools imported via the registry through Apogee or first-party MCP servers. Hybrid orchestration is being introduced to enable agents to call other agents regardless of whether they are ADK or low-code/no-code, and skills will serve as a connective, abstract layer to simplify calling multiple agents or tools.
                           * Skills Management System (Project Manta): The skills management and authoring system (referred to as Project Manta) will act as a central repository to flatten the overall agent hierarchy by allowing a skill to reference multiple agents and tools. This system is already in Early Access and will be launched for public preview around Cloud Next.
                           * Clarification on Agent Gateway versus Apogee: Vamsi Ramakrishnan clarified the distinction between the Agent Gateway and Apogee, noting that the Agent Gateway is primarily an identity-based system that connects agents to agents and agents to tools, but it does not handle hosting of MCP servers or REST APIs. Apogee, however, provides broader capabilities, including a developer portal and the ability to mediate and convert REST or SOAP APIs into MCP action-based JSON RPC resources. Both systems are managed by the same product management group and use similar underlying technology.
                           * Agent Gateway Extensibility and Registries: The Agent Gateway provides extensibility through service extensions, allowing custom code execution to enforce policies, such as filtering PII or triggering model armor. Bookkeeping is managed through the Tool Registry (for tools) and the Agent Registry (for agents), which is a successor to the container registry that allows provisioning of permissions, which are enforced on the gateway.
                           * Deep Research API Launch on Vertex AI: Vamsi Ramakrishnan confirmed that the Deep Research API service, based on the Interactions API, will be launched as a standalone service on Vertex AI around Cloud Next. This capability will allow the uploading of private files for deep research and, in the next phase, will support querying private APIs via MCP. This new API simplifies architecture by offering a single endpoint for planning and execution, and it can be used to augment or replace the existing Google Search grounding tool for customers like SEEK.

Summary
G Demo Generator training covered custom demonstration creation for specific customer challenges via realistic data sets and automated deployment.


G Demo Generator Functionality
Ryota Tokodai presented the G Demo Generator, an App Script tool that creates custom product demonstrations by synthesizing setup scripts and customized data sets on 5 default BigQuery tables. The tool utilizes the Gemini 3.1 Pro model to fetch real-world information, creating realistic data and custom scenarios tailored to customer-specific challenges.


Demo Deployment and Setup
The demo is deployed by running a long shell script in the cloud shell terminal window, which automates IAM roles and permissions management. This script offers 3 deployment options: local ADK web, public Cloud Run, or deployment to a Gemini Enterprise instance, and includes a cleanup command to destroy all created resources.


Environment and Future Scope
The Argolis environment is the recommended environment for running the script, though Nexus projects with a 90-day limited billing account are an option. A future update will enable the agent to write back and update a database using Firestore for real-time database visualization.

Details
                           * Introduction and Recording Consent: The meeting began with introductions, and Jordan France requested and received confirmation to record the session. Rob sent apologies for being pulled into a hygiene meeting.
                           * SMB Team Affiliation and New Starter Welcome: Jacob Mukkada and Ryota Tokodai confirmed they are both on the SMB team, with Jacob Mukkada based in Sydney and Jordan France in Melbourne.
                           * G Demo Generator Overview and Purpose: Ryota Tokodai introduced the G Demo Generator, a tool for creating custom product demonstrations for individual customers, accessed via the go/g-demo-generator link and hosted on App Script.
                           * Custom Demo Generation Process: The generator synthesizes a complete setup script and a customized data set, provisioning data on five default BigQuery tables based on a business challenge scenario input. The resulting data set is intended to be realistic, avoiding standardized, generic scenarios found in pre-made demos.
                           * Realistic Data Customization: The generator uses a Gemini 3.1 Pro model to fetch real-world information, allowing it to create realistic data sets like store names or branches if a major brand or customer name is input. Industry and job function templates populate standardized text, but users are encouraged to use free text for customer-specific challenges.
                           * Tracking and Time Considerations: To track usage, users input their vector opportunity ID or select "personal learning". The generation process takes about six or seven minutes due to a long-run Gemini code generation. The tool displays a history of demo generations and a community tab where generated scripts from other Googlers are archived.
                           * Utilizing Archived Demo Scripts: Users can save time by clicking "load demo" on an archived script to load the history completely, skipping the lengthy generation time. The system prompt for the ADK agent, including realistic factors like shopping habits, is available for review, as is the data structure detailing the five interconnected BigQuery tables.
                           * Deployment Process via Shell Script: To start the demo, users must copy a long shell script and paste it into their cloud shell terminal window. The shell script contains all necessary resources for running the demo.
                           * Deployment Options and Cleanup: Executing the script offers three deployment options: local ADK web deployment, a public Cloud Run instance with a URL, or deployment to a Gemini Enterprise instance. The script automates necessary groundwork, including managing IAM roles and permissions. The shell script includes a bash [script name] --cleanup command to destroy all resources created.
                           * Gemini Enterprise Registration Automation: Pedro Correia confirmed that the script automates the registration of the ADK agent into the Gemini Enterprise environment. This uses standardized commands within the agent starter pack, such as make deploy and make register.
                           * Generated Demo Materials and Scenarios: The generator produces demo materials, including a PDF file and an Excel file for Gemini analysis (relevant for an AWT concept). It also creates a series of realistic demo scenarios tailored to specific personas, like a CTO, with complex prompts to showcase the agent's capabilities.
                           * Agent Behavior and Data Digging: Ryota Tokodai demonstrated that the agent produces status updates and SQL queries as it actively analyzes the BigQuery data. The agent starts by running simple SQL queries to understand the data structure, even without prior knowledge of the tables or data.
                           * Agent Registration to Existing Gemini Enterprise Instances: If only one Gemini Enterprise instance exists, the script uses it; if there are multiple, the user is prompted to select which one to deploy to. The script does not spin up a new Gemini Enterprise instance.
                           * Project Deployment Requirements: Pedro Correia confirmed that users need to set the default project using the gcloud config set project command before running the script to designate the target project for resource deployment. Michael Scutt was advised to use their Argolis project or ensure owner/editor permissions in a shared project to run the Cloud Shell script.
                           * Recommended Environments and Billing Considerations: The Argolis environment is recommended for running the script. Nexus (nex.corp.google.com) is an option for creating fresh GCB projects with a 90-day limited billing account. A caveat of the Nexus project is that it only allows for a 30-day trial of the paid Gemini Enterprise license.
                           * Future Enhancements to the Tool: Ryota Tokodai announced a future major update that will enable the agent to write back and update a database using Firestore, allowing users to see the database update in real-time.

[Confirmed] Google <> THE ICONIC UCP Kickoff
Description: Meeting confirmed Australia as a priority launch market for Google's agentic commerce experiences with a focus on clarifying Universal Commerce Protocol (UCP) details and native checkout functionality. Google's agentic vision aims to be an essential shopping partner by connecting merchants and consumers via native agentic experiences, not acting as a retailer. The UCP is an open-source standard linking merchant backends to agentic surfaces, ensuring merchants remain the merchant of record and control customer relationships. The native checkout experience uses Google Pay profiles for rapid transactions, with Google passing user information to the retailer for order fulfillment and showing an order success screen. Products are opted-in via a supplemental feed, and multi-retailer comparisons offer buy buttons for UCP-enabled retailers, merchandising universal attributes like price and speed. The implementation plan involves four stages, focusing the bulk of the 30-day timeline on development, testing, and validation of native checkout endpoints, targeting a launch date in mid-May.

Ateendees: natalia.pawlak@theiconic.com.au, melissa.zinman@theiconic.com.au, jpgovela@google.com, sarahjane@google.com, glynn.willis@theiconic.com.au, erin.mauger@theiconic.com.au, krista.wright@theiconic.com.au, theresa.payne@theiconic.com.au, ankitanagpal@google.com, amyrom@google.com, cheungr@google.com, jingyli@google.com, pcorreia@google.com, rjerrems@google.com, ray.zhang@theiconic.com.au, rshagun@google.com, parthasundaram@google.com


Meeting notes: Meeting Notes Document


Notes:


Summary
Meeting confirmed Australia as a priority launch market for Google's agentic commerce experiences with a focus on clarifying Universal Commerce Protocol details and native checkout functionality.


Agentic Vision and UCP Standard
Google's agentic vision aims to be an essential shopping partner by connecting merchants and consumers via native agentic experiences, not acting as a retailer. The Universal Commerce Protocol is an open-source standard linking merchant backends to agentic surfaces, ensuring merchants remain the merchant of record and control customer relationships.


Native Checkout and Fulfillment
The native checkout experience uses Google Pay profiles for rapid transactions, with Google passing user information to the retailer for order fulfillment and showing an order success screen. Products are opted-in via a supplemental feed, and multi-retailer comparisons offer buy buttons for UCP-enabled retailers, merchandising universal attributes like price and speed.


Launch Implementation Plan
The implementation plan involves 4 stages, with the bulk of the 30-day timeline focused on development, testing, and validation of native checkout endpoints. The target launch date is mid-May, requiring immediate internal coordination with technical teams on order management and cart systems.


Next steps
                           * [ ] [Sarah-Jane Campbell] Share Notes: Distribute meeting notes to participants after the session.
                           * [ ] [Partha Sundaram] Review Address Form: Check Australia payments address field support and send details to The group. Compare Google address form against the iconic site form; acknowledge any major delta.
                           * [ ] [Someone in Lost (au-syd-pir)] Send Resources: Follow up post-call with the presentation deck and all relevant resources. Send a project tracker detailing next steps and milestones.
                           * [ ] [Someone in Lost (au-syd-pir)] Provide Contacts: Send contact information immediately after the call to enable quick answers.
                           * [ ] [Someone in Lost (au-syd-pir)] Follow Up: Contact The group by Monday or Tuesday to get an assessment update.
                           * [ ] [The group] Assess Feed Health: Google teams will assess the iconic feed current state. Highlight any blockers or additional work required.


Details
                           * Meeting Kick-off and Program Overview: Australia is a priority launch market for Google's agenda commerce experiences. The purpose was to clarify details about the native checkout experience powered by the Universal Commerce Protocol (UCP). The goal was to confirm The Iconic's internal alignment for progression as a launch partner.
                           * Google's Agentic Vision and Core Principles: Google's agentic vision is to be an essential partner in shopping journeys by connecting merchants and consumers through best-in-class native agentic shopping experiences. Core principles are: Google is not a retailer, they act as a matchmaker connecting merchants and consumers, and they will enable new experiences by showcasing personalized products.
                           * Understanding the Universal Commerce Protocol (UCP): UCP is an open-source, flexible commerce standard linking merchant backends to agentic consumer surfaces like Google Search, AI mode, and Gemini. It provides a common language for agents, platforms, and businesses to communicate without bespoke connections. The protocol ensures merchants remain in control of their business promise and customer relationship, serving as the merchant of record.
                           * Scope and Functionality of UCP: UCP provides for an end-to-end shopping journey, covering product discovery, consideration, purchase, and post-purchase. It is also interoperable with other protocols like A2A (agent-to-agent) and AP2.
                           * Identity and Account Linking: Account linking is built on an open Google identity Request for Comments (RFC) and links a user's Google account with their account on the retailer's site. The system also supports guest checkout for retailers who offer it, which bypasses the need for account linking.
                           * The Native Checkout Experience Overview: The experience starts with a product viewer on Google's surface. When the user taps "buy," the system uses preloaded information from their Google Pay (GP) profile, such as payment methods and shipping address, for a quick checkout.
                           * Merchant of Record and Order Fulfillment: The retailer remains the merchant of record from a payments perspective. Google passes user information to the retailer for order fulfillment and shows an order success screen. Google is also building light order history experiences for users.
                           * Product Feed Requirements and Selection: The product feed remains critical. Retailers can upload a supplemental feed to designate which products are eligible for these agentic experiences. This allows retailers to exclude complex products or those requiring specific business logic.
                           * Multi-Retailer Comparison and Merchant USPs: Google offers a multi-retailer comparison experience in the product viewer, where a buy button is available for UCP-enabled retailers. Annotations like "free delivery" and "30-day returns" merchandise the product, leading with universal attributes like price and speed.
                           * UCP's Role in Checkout Data and Current Limitations: The final checkout screens are powered in real-time by UCP API calls to the retailer's backend, with the retailer remaining the source of truth for data like shipping options. Currently, product support is limited to single-item checkout.
                           * Handling Product Variants and Item IDs: Variants (like size and color) are captured through the feed and displayed in the product viewer. When checkout is initiated, Google sends an item ID, drawn from the retailer's feed specification, back over UCP.
                           * Delivery Options and Limitations: Delivery options are pulled from the user's GP profile and then sent to the retailer, who returns a list of delivery options via UCP. Currently, the system only supports home deliveries and does not support scheduled delivery, pickup, parcel lockers, or post office boxes. Partha Sundaram offered to follow up offline to compare the retailer's address form with Google's.
                           * Payment and Authentication Flow Details: The current MVP identity linking flow is only enabled for signed-in Google users and links their Google account to their Iconic account. While UCP is an open specification, Google's current implementation starts with the Google Pay payment handler only, which supports various payment types and can work with payment service providers like Stripe.
                           * Loyalty Program Integration: Google recently launched loyalty solutions built on account linking that display member benefits. However, The Iconic's points-based loyalty program is currently not compatible with Google's existing loyalty product.
                           * Launch Timelines and Next Steps: The deployment completion and process alignment are needed within the next 30 days. The target launch date is mid-May, highlighting Australia's critical role as an international partner. Google will follow up early next week to get a sense of the retailer's progression.
                           * The Four-Stage Implementation Plan: Jorge outlined the stages: (1) Kickoff Discussion, (2) Merchant Center Preparation (supplemental feed setup), (3) Development Work (publishing UCP profile, building native checkout endpoints, payments, and post-order purchase events), and (4) Testing, Validation, and Launch. Stages three and four are estimated to take the bulk of the time (around one month).
                           * Required Stakeholders and Feed Assessment: Required teams include technical teams handling the order management system, checkout, and cart, and coordination with the feed provider (like Products Up). The Google team will assess the current feed.
                           * Decision Deadline and Commitment: Glynn Willis asked for an assessment update by early the following week. The launch is self-selecting due to the aggressive timeline, and the retailer's expertise makes them a suitable partner.

Summary
The meeting introduced Tally Group initiatives and defined architectural consulting needs for the Adora AI platform launch. Tally Group is currently migrating infrastructure to Google Cloud Platform and developing agentic AI solutions for energy sector billing. The Adora AI initiative aims to automate CRM tasks and disrupt energy retail through advanced backend API orchestration. The group decided to conduct a preliminary session with technical leadership to establish foundational best practices for the platform. The goal of this engagement is to showcase a primary use case within 8 to 10 weeks.


Next steps
                           * [Courtney McWatt] Share Framework: Distribute Tally Group framework documentation after the meeting.
                           * [Courtney McWatt] Distribute Notes: Send meeting notes to relevant participants.

Details
                           * Introduction of Pedro Correia's Role and Tally Group Overview: Pedro Correia is dedicated to the tech assess team. Tally Group is a large, little-known provider of SAS software offering end-to-end services (including billing and account management) to the energy and utilities market. They are engaged in two streams of work: migrating their Azure and AWS environments to GCP, and performing agentic AI work.
                           * Demonstration of the High Bill Use Case: The first agentic AI use case addresses customers receiving unexpectedly high bills, one of the most common contact center call types. This task previously took a human agent around 20 minutes to resolve manually. The new solution uses an agent assist system with Gemini to analyze data and provide a comprehensive explanation in about 30 seconds, reducing the total call time to around five minutes.
                           * The Adora AI Orchestration Platform and Vision: Tally Group is packaging its AI-driven capabilities into a service called Adora AI, their master orchestration platform for building multiple specialized agents. Adora AI is envisioned to range from telling agents how to perform tasks, to showing them, and eventually doing the tasks for them (e.g., updating a phone number in a CRM system). The ultimate vision is to disrupt the energy retail sector by removing the human agent and replacing the CRM screen with backend API calls driven entirely by the AI agent.
                           * Strategic Importance and Need for Foundational Guidance: The Adora AI initiative is the number one priority across Tally Group, driven by the CTO, Eric. Tally Group is going to market soon with Adora AI, having found early retail partners. They are seeking guidance to ensure the underlying architecture (data, governance, and security layers) is built according to best practices to avoid future re-engineering.
                           * Tally Group's Current Technical Status and Goals: Tally Group is currently migrating their billing engine from Azure to GCP. They have built foundational migration components, including an API layer powered by Apigee. They also developed a common data layer over the last 12 months to act as a single source of truth across their six billing engines. Their strategy is to lead with the new Adora agents as "loss leaders" rather than directly selling the billing engine.
                           * Next Steps for Architecture Consultation: The team agreed the next step is a preliminary online session with Eric Gilmaz, the AI lead at Tally. The purpose is to review the existing product and gather requirements for the target state. The team will then offer guidance on which architectural components can be confidently built now versus those subject to change due to the evolving nature of agent-to-agent technology.
                           * Coordination of the Initial Session: George Tzoumanekas committed to coordinating the initial introductory session with Eric and aligning Pedro Correia. The preference is for the initial session to be online, aiming to work fast to showcase a use case at Summit within eight to ten weeks.

Notes:
The overall goal of the Gemini Enterprise Adoption plan for Temple & Webster is to shift the deployment from a low confidence, low value state to a high value, high satisfaction environment.


The plan focuses on three key areas:
                              * 1. Agent Development Kit (ADK) & Technical Enablement
                              * Goal: Accelerate agent development and establish agent building best practices to facilitate a broader rollout at T&W.
                              * Key Activities: Conduct joint sprints for customer ADK agent development with CEs and AI specialists. T&W needs to assign an AI platform engineer as a Point of Contact (PoC) to help oversee guardrails advised by Google.
                              * 2. Code Assist & Developer Productivity
                              * Goal: Educate developers on the practical uses of the tool to maximize engineering efficiency and reliability.
                              * Key Activities: Provide deep-dive training on what is possible with Code Assist and how to use the tools effectively. Provide instructions on how to use Code Assist through GE licenses.
                              * 3. Business User Enablement (Agent Builder)
                              * Goal: Empower non-technical users to build low-code solutions using the Agent Builder while establishing platform governance.
                              * Key Activities: Conduct a hands-on workshop with business users on actual use cases, building solutions together using the Agent Builder. Provide initial education (Level Setting) on Gemini Enterprise, including its function and how it differs from Workspace.
The Champions Group Program includes forming a "mini champions group" with representatives from areas like HR and Marketing to drive early adoption and value.


The Champions Group activities are:
                              * A dedicated Champions Kickoff session to provide a Gemini Enterprise introduction and level set expectations.
                              * An Agent Building Exercise in a "hackathon-style" format to generate excitement and build initial, low-risk use cases, aiming to prevent the proliferation of unmanaged agents.
                              * Scheduling recurrent progress reviews (e.g., possibly bi-weekly meetings with Rima) to offer hands-on assistance and ensure the platform remains governed as it scales.
Proposed Next Steps & Communication timeline:
                              * Company-wide GE Overview & Kick Off - Week 1
                              * Concurrent Pillar Kick-Offs & Workshops - Week 2-4
                              * Formation of Sprints/Milestones - Week 2-4
                              * Check Point (Mike & Rima, assessment of progress) - 6 weeks
                              * Recurrent Monthly progress Reviews - 8 weeks onwards

Summary
Focusing on agent-based retailer operations for a reduced cost to serve, the discussion detailed 3 agent domains for AI-native platforms, including architectural and data foundations.


Agent Vision and Cost
The vision for retailer operations involves AI-native platforms using interconnected agents to achieve substantial cost reductions, targeting 70% to 80% with combined AI. Critical protections must be implemented to prevent regulatory breaches in highly regulated markets while pursuing high automation.


Defined Agent Domains
3 agent domains were defined: Adora Insight for real-time call intelligence, Adora Companion for operator assistance and guided work instructions, and Defender for silent compliance and auditing actions. These agents aim to reduce call center churn and improve cost to serve by minimizing the time needed for operator competency.


Architecture, Data, and Goals
The model, which includes the Predict domain, is expected to deliver a 30% reduction in average handling time and nearly 100% compliance via channel-agnostic agents. Establishing a consistent foundational architecture with a prime agent, a robust data layer in GCP, and agent management tools is required to support this multi-agent system.


Suggested next steps
                              * Plan Workshop: Conduct internal synchronization meeting to prepare architectural steps and platform road map details (Pedro Correia, Jaco Silvis, George Tzoumanekas).
                              * Coordinate Workshop: Lock in follow-up workshop time for Eric Yilmaz and K Pala, targeting end of next week (George Tzoumanekas).

                              * Notable announcements include the top-tier Gemini 3.1 TTS flash model and the Gemini Embedding 2 model.

                              * 1MCP for Agents and Low-Code/No-Code Tools
                              * The 1MCP system can be onboarded as a connector via Bring Your Own MCP (BYO MCP).
                              * This allows Gemini Enterprise agents and low-code/no-code agents, such as stitch or Maps Grounded, to use it.
                              * This integration provides a direct interface for tools like Cloud SQL, moving beyond simple search and retrieval to include actions like updating data.

                              * The capability to use remote MCP servers allows for executing SQL statements and performing administrative tasks as a studio user.

                              * The ability to use imagery in a Q&A system is expected to be useful for industries like insurance.
                              * Image grounding can reduce a retrieval hop by using Google image search grounding in the retrieval stage.
                              * This is useful for brand-aware content creation and leveraging Google Maps imagery for business places.
                              * Skill Feature Testing and Orchestration Role
                              * Skills are intended to become the glue for orchestration, allowing agents to reference other agents for complex workflows.

                              * Vamsi Ramakrishnan previously built a standalone equivalent of a Gemini Enterprise claims processing agent using the Gemini CLI and Firebase.

Kogan only operates online and does not have stores.

Ateendees: michaelscutt@google.com joeriovart@google.com russnash@google.com emclucas@google.com rsibo@google.com pcorreia@google.com marygourley@google.com smansfield@google.com
Meeting notes:


Notes:
The presentation notes include context and talking points for several slides:
                              * Partnership Ecosystem & Momentum (Slide 8): Joeri needs to add a slide regarding the Meso current partnership.
                              * Google's Differentiators (Slide 4, 20): Google’s capabilities include getting access to world-leading breakthroughs faster, replacing disparate tools, protecting the business with unmatched visibility, and flexibly combining innovations with existing tools. Differentiated search, multimodality built into Gemini models, and personalization grounded in Workspace, Google Search, and a catalog of connectors are key features.
                              * Strategic Initiatives (Slide 17): The initiatives expand on four themes heard from the customer. Building out Sky’s cloud-native digital capability is considered the foundation. Other themes include AI-powered Smart Analytics, modernization of the media supply chain, and creation of an empowered workforce.
                              * AI Functions (Slide 31): The hype surrounding AI is driven by both technology advances and changes to organizational pressures and expectations.
                              * AI-powered Search (Slide 41): This enables Semantic Knowledge Base searches for support agents and Customer Similarity Engines to improve cross-selling/up-selling strategies.
                              * Customer Experience (CX) (Slides 50-52):
                              * Customer expectations are elevated due to AI tools, demanding personalized results, "agentic" engagement (actions done on their behalf), and immersive experiences.
                              * Most brand interactions still suffer from an "experience gap," leading 82% of consumers to avoid a website after a single poor experience.
                              * The goal is to shift from reactive service to proactive loyalty by enabling hyper-personalized information retrieval and self-guided troubleshooting, anchoring the narrative in a unique Value Wedge.
                              * Gemini Enterprise for CX (Slides 53, 58, 59):
                              * This solution transforms fragmented interactions into a seamless customer journey, aiming to grow revenue, drive loyalty, and reduce operational costs.
                              * The platform is low-code and visual, using over 35 pre-built templates and 100+ connectors (e.g., Salesforce, ServiceNow) to reduce development time and ensure seamless handoff between AI and humans.
                              * It delivers a signature AI experience the customer owns and controls, ensuring their data is not used to train foundation models.
                              * The Agent Advantage Demo (Slide 63): The objective is to demonstrate reasoning, data, and action together, showcasing a digital employee that can reason through a trade-in, plan financing, and autonomously book a test drive.
                              * Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform (Slides 74, 77):
                              * The platform unifies tailored user surfaces with the core Agent Platform, sitting on top of Google Cloud’s integrated AI stack (AI Hypercomputer, Agentic Data Cloud, and Agent Defense).
                              * Key components include access to Google’s best models (The Brains), no-code tools for employees (The Workbench), pre-built/partner agents (The Taskforce), connectivity to company data (The Context), centralized control (The Governance), and an extensive Partner Ecosystem.
                              * Agent Features & Roadmap (Slides 82-88):
                              * Long-running Agents (Preview at Next ‘26): Agents that handle complex tasks over hours or days in an isolated sandbox, supporting ad hoc, event-triggered, and scheduled tasks (Speaker: Maryam).
                              * Inbox (Preview at Next ‘26): Allows employees to manage agents through a Gemini Enterprise inbox or notifications for task completion, deep research, or required human action (Speaker: Maryam).
                              * Enterprise Security: Generally Available features include Model Armor Integration to protect against prompt injections and data leakages. Sensitive Data Protection (PII Detection policy) is in Preview.
                              * Business Impact (Slide 93): Customer references include:
                              * Signal Iduna: Reduced complex health insurance processing time by 37% and dropped escalations from 27% to 3%.
                              * Anaplan: Achieved 80% adoption rate with 1500+ employee-created agents.
                              * Macquarie: Saved 100,000+ hours with 100+ agents created.
                              * Walmart (Slide 99): Walmart is deploying Gemini Enterprise globally to empower 1M+ associates and regional leaders, improving productivity by providing actionable insights in seconds via Pixel Fold devices connected to enterprise data.
                              * Deployment (Slide 102): Day 1 out-of-the-box (OOTB) capabilities include a secure chat experience, Notebook LM, and no-code/pre-built agents. Early Months include search across 3P enterprise data and connected functional agents.

CEPF Update for APAC CEs/PAs [Option 2]
Description: Hi all! This session will cover the new CEPF requirements for all CE and Principal Architect roles.


Attachments: drive link


Ateendees:
Meeting notes:


Notes:
                                 * CEPF Requirements for Principal Architects (PA) in Enterprise Architecture (EA):
                                 * L200 requirements include passing the Professional Cloud Architect Certification and completing Professional Skills Training - Business Value Discovery.
                                 * L300 requirements (prerequisite of L200 in EA) include completing any Challenge Lab, Advanced Professional Skills Training, and PA Packet Attestation.
                                 * The PA Packet Attestation requires specific artifacts: a Technical Account Plan, Two Detailed Case Studies, a Client Reference, and demonstrated leadership.
                                 * CEPF Requirements for CE Roles (General):
                                 * L200 requirements typically include a Google Cloud Certification mapped to the Primary Practice, Professional Skills, and Foundational Code Capability (WIP).
                                 * L300 requirements typically include Challenge Labs, Packet Attestation by Field SME, and Advanced Professional Skills.
                                 * CEPF Delivery Schedule (Q2 2026 Focus):
                                 * L300 Challenge Labs for Platform CEs and Outcome CEs launched in Q1-Q2 2026 (March-April).
                                 * The Challenge Lab portfolios for Outcome CEs and Platform CEs will be published in Q2 2026. CEs will have three months to complete additional labs once launched.
                                 * L300 Packet Attestation for Platform CEs and Outcome CEs is scheduled for Q2 2026.
                                 * L400 Packet Attestation for Practice CEs is scheduled for Q2 2026.
                                 * Completion Timelines for Existing CEs (Reset Jan ‘26):
                                 * L200 must be completed by August 31, 2026.
                                 * Partial L300 (excluding packet) must be completed by September 30, 2026.
                                 * Full L300 (including packet) must be completed by July 31, 2027.
                                 * Start Date Impact for Tenure Calculations:
                                 * A lateral team move into a position with changes to Practice Area or Core Role, or transferring into a CE position from a non-CE position, resets the official CE Start Date to the Transfer Date.
                                 * If there are no changes to Practice Area or Core Role, the Original Start Date is maintained.

Meeting notes:


Notes:
The following key notes were extracted from the AuNZ CE Team Huddle presentation deck:
                                    * AlphaFold 3 is a major evolution from AlphaFold 2, modeling all biological molecule interactions (proteins, DNA, RNA, ligands, ions, etc.), and its predictions surpass existing specialized systems. It uses an improved Evoformer module and a diffusion network.
                                    * BigQuery Markup Language (BQML) workloads are almost always better run on Editions (slots-based) pricing rather than on-demand to prevent accidental overspend. One customer accidentally spent $2.4M across two days due to running BQML jobs on on-demand pricing.
                                    * Cultural Content: It is traditional in New Zealand to begin meetings with a Karakia (a secular incantation aimed at grounding and focusing), complementing the Australian tradition of acknowledging the country.
                                    * Dev Competition: The Simplify & Accelerate: The GCP DX Challenge competition runs from July 21st to August 15th. Award categories include "Flow Master," "AI Innovator," and "Problem Solver," with prizes planned for winners.
                                    * GenSec AI is a capability that infuses AI into security workflows using Gemini and SecLM, a specialized Large Language Model trained on threat intelligence.
                                    * Google Unified Security is a platform that converges Google's core security offerings—threat visibility, SecOps, cloud security, and Mandiant expertise—powered by Gemini AI and running on the Security Data Fabric. Mandiant consulting offers services in Incident Response, Strategic Readiness, Offensive Security, and Cybersecurity Transformation.
                                    * Outcome CE (OCE) Team: The CE organization is shifting from a consultative model to a focused execution structure. The OCE team's mandate is to accelerate customer value realization and revenue growth by ensuring the rapid ramp of committed workloads into production.
                                    * Web Risk Policy: For API submission, the minimum deal size is $100K. Deals between $100K and $250K only receive CE support for Proof of Concept (POC), while deals over $250K receive CE support plus engineering support.
                                    * Workload Identity Federation (WLIF): Since early 2024, an enhancement was introduced allowing users to optionally skip the step of impersonating a GCP Service Account when configuring WLIF.

Jasper / Pedro
Description: Adora AI questions from Tally Group


Attachments: Adora AI - POC 2 & 3


Ateendees: wangjasper@google.com, pcorreia@google.com
Meeting notes:


Notes:
                                       * POC 2: Predictive Messaging
                                       * The objective is to use electricity meter data to work out whether a customer has an electric vehicle (EV) and/or a home battery.
                                       * Meter data is recorded in 30-minute intervals and analyzed in consecutive 28-day snapshots.
                                       * For each 28-day window, the analysis captures whether an EV was present (Yes/No), whether a home battery might be relevant (Yes/No), and an estimate of electricity usage related to EV charging.
                                       * The outreach process involves computing gaps, creating tiered outreach based on detected EV usage, and running a thin pilot to monitor conversion and complaints.
                                       * POC 3: Weather-Based Alerting
                                       * This project correlates daily outdoor temperature with daily energy usage to understand how consumption changes when it is hot or cold.
                                       * Customers are segmented by usage history: Gold (typically 2 years or less) and Platinum (more than 2 years).
                                       * Temperature triggers for heating or cooling are derived based on historical weather and usage patterns.
                                       * The system outputs a file containing customer identifiers, classification strategy, and temperature triggers (summer/heat and/or winter/cold).
                                       * POC 4: Real-Time AQM (Defender)
                                       * This project aims to prevent regulatory breaches by transcribing and analyzing live calls.
                                       * Focus areas include Wrongful Disconnections and compliance with the Payment Difficulty Framework.
                                       * The AI compares customer-requested dates against data entered in the CRM by agents to flag discrepancies for timely follow-up.
                                       * The ultimate goal is real-time alerting to prevent breaches before the call ends.
                                       * Conversational Flow (GECX)
                                       * The AI assistant is designed to help with electricity bill and usage inquiries, provide general advice on solar and batteries, and submit gas meter reads.
                                       * Key tools for the assistant include AccountDetails (for account information), AccountInvoices (for querying billing data), and CDL-ElecUsage-Midlayer (for high-level electricity usage values).
                                       * When explaining changes in bill amounts, the assistant follows a structured conversational flow: comparing bills, analyzing usage patterns, providing estimated appliance-level breakdowns, and offering advice on reducing usage.


Tally Group // Quick Sync # 2


Description: The technical meeting focused on data migration strategies, infrastructure optimization (specifically leveraging BigQuery and Data Stream), and planning the next steps for the energy usage analytics pipeline.


Attachments: Meeting Document


Ateendees: bhagya.rupasinghe@tally-group.com, jacosilvis@google.com


Meeting notes: The team decided to move away from CSV files to Parquet and Avro for migration efficiency. They identified using BigQuery global queries as the solution for accessing US-based weather data from the Australia-based environment. Additionally, they discussed using PubSub and Cloud Run functions for cost-calculation logic and scheduling a follow-up session for Monday at 10:30 AM to track progress.


Notes:


Summary
Technical discussion addressed data migration strategy and optimized infrastructure integration for energy usage analytics.


Migration and Infrastructure Strategy
Parquet and Avro formats replaced Comma Separated Values files to optimize data migration efficiency. Teams adopted Data Stream for real-time synchronization via hybrid cloud storage.


Pipeline and Regional Connectivity
BigQuery global queries resolved regional storage mismatches preventing data joins. This enabled cross-region analysis without moving sensitive datasets.


Orchestration and Model Development
BigQuery and Cloud Run functions were selected for cost calculation logic. BigQuery will trigger PubSub messages to optimize processing speeds for energy consumption models.


Decisions


ALIGNED
                                       * Global query usage for cross-region data: The team aligned on using BigQuery global queries as the strategy to resolve the issue of accessing US-based weather data from the Australia-based environment.


Next steps
                                       * [ ] [Bhagya Rupasinghe] Map Postcodes: Update the postcode to latitude and longitude mapping within the data pipeline using the mapping generated by the analytics agent.
                                       * [ ] [Bhagya Rupasinghe] Run Pipeline: Run the data pipeline with the global BigQuery query and the new mapping to verify successful execution.
                                       * [ ] [Bhagya Rupasinghe] Prepare Data: Prepare the current data state to include interval usage data and pricing information for model development.
                                       * [ ] [Bhagya Rupasinghe] Communicate Blockers: Notify Jaco Silvis via message if any issues arise during data execution or if technical blockers occur before the next meeting.
                                       * [ ] [Bhagya Rupasinghe] Migrate Data: Perform data migration for subsequent pipeline steps after the current migration is successful.
                                       * [ ] [The group] Schedule Meeting: Schedule a follow-up meeting for Monday at 10:30 AM to discuss the pipeline progress and brainstorm the email notification logic.


Details
                                       * Data Migration Challenges and Process Improvements: Bhagya Rupasinghe explained that a migration script ran for three days, leading to a Google authentication token expiration before the tables could be merged ([00:00:14]). To resolve this, Bhagya Rupasinghe adopted a new process of retrieving the query, saving it as a Comma Separated Values file, and then performing the migration to avoid long-running script timeouts. Jaco Silvis advised against using Comma Separated Values files, noting that they often cause import issues, and recommended using Parquet or Avro formats instead for better compression and schema encapsulation ([00:01:37]).
                                       * Future Data Synchronization and Infrastructure: Bhagya Rupasinghe noted that they are currently using a Python script for extraction but expressed interest in more robust solutions for a production-ready implementation ([00:02:38]). Jaco Silvis suggested exploring Data Stream, a service that sits on top of PostgreSQL Change Data Capture logs to stream data to BigQuery in near real-time ([00:03:35]). Jaco Silvis advised that while this is efficient and reduces operational overhead, the team should test it for production to ensure costs remain manageable depending on the volume of database changes. Bhagya Rupasinghe expressed a preference for migrating data into Google Cloud Storage buckets based on previous advice, and Jaco Silvis suggested a hybrid approach where bulk loads occur via Amazon Simple Storage Service and Google Cloud Storage, while Data Stream handles real-time synchronization ([00:04:46]).
                                       * Regional Data Discrepancies and Pipeline Issues: Bhagya Rupasinghe described issues with a pipeline that correlates energy usage with weather data, noting that the pipeline initially failed due to the merge taking excessive time and a mismatch in regional storage. Specifically, the energy usage data was migrated to Australia, while the weather data remained in the United States, preventing them from performing necessary joins ([00:07:07]). Jaco Silvis suggested exploring BigQuery global queries, which allow users to query data across regions without needing to move the data, provided the configuration is enabled ([00:08:58]).
                                       * Mapping Weather Data for Regional Queries: Bhagya Rupasinghe needs to create a mapping between postal codes and latitude/longitude coordinates to correctly integrate the weather data. Bhagya Rupasinghe plans to use a publicly available dataset for this purpose and will use the Analytics Agent to assist with the mapping ([00:10:07], [00:16:24]). Jaco Silvis demonstrated the BigQuery Data Transfer Service as an alternative to copy entire datasets between regions, though they encountered a permissions error during the demonstration ([00:11:45], [00:15:16]). Bhagya Rupasinghe will proceed with the global query approach and update Jaco Silvis if the implementation is successful ([00:16:24]).
                                       * Schema Expectations for Model Development: Bhagya Rupasinghe outlined the schema goals, noting that Model 1 should output customer-specific sensitive temperatures for heating and cooling ([00:20:03]). Model 2 requires interval-based energy data, specifically 5-minute intervals, to identify usage patterns. The overall goal of these models is to predict next-day temperature usage, identify customers who might be high users of specific appliances, and estimate energy savings ([00:21:40]).
                                       * Orchestration and Cloud Run Functions: Bhagya Rupasinghe discussed the plan to use a Cloud Run function to calculate estimated costs and determine if an email should be sent to a customer based on savings exceeding 10 dollars ([00:23:11], [00:26:56]). Jaco Silvis proposed that BigQuery can trigger a PubSub message, which would then trigger the Cloud Run service to perform these calculations. Jaco Silvis suggested that if the process involves interacting with external APIs, such as pricing data, Cloud Run is an appropriate tool; however, if the logic can be contained entirely within BigQuery, it may be faster and more cost-effective to perform the processing there ([00:24:24], [00:29:07]).
                                       * Meeting Schedule and Next Steps: Bhagya Rupasinghe and Jaco Silvis agreed to meet again on Monday at 10:30 AM to review progress ([00:30:36]). Bhagya Rupasinghe plans to finalize the data migration and pipeline implementation before the meeting ([00:29:07]). Mitchell Stanford noted that they expect to have more time available to assist with these tasks before the Monday session ([00:30:36]).

Shopping Agent - Commerce AI product overview for AU teams


Description: Hello Gretchen and AU Team (Invited Retail sellers, AISS and AICE but open for anyone to join),


I've set aside some 1-1 time with Gretchen Kingry for some personal enablement on Agentic Commerce and the shopping agent, you're all welcome to join.


Key areas to cover:
                                       * Product overview, vision/roadmap
                                       * Commercial model
                                       * Delivery. Qualification and readiness, partner/FDE options
                                       * AOB
Thanks
JB


Attachments: Meeting Document


Ateendees: jessicaaa@google.com, jonmimo@google.com, harisrinivasan@google.com, michaelscutt@google.com, chriscrampton@google.com, mccrohan@google.com, jonnyburgess@google.com, rodwilliams@google.com, joeriovart@google.com, gchami@google.com, hwk@google.com, gregharrison@google.com, gkingry@google.com, cguzzetti@google.com, rsibo@google.com, dfernandezcao@google.com, pcorreia@google.com, amyrom@google.com


Meeting notes:


Notes:


Summary
The team reviewed shopping agent capabilities and integration strategies for enhancing retailer engagement via product updates.


Product Capabilities and Strategy
Retailers achieved high interaction rates through consumer education on shopping agent features like virtual try-ons. Leadership is currently overhauling pricing strategies to improve customer adoption ROI.


Roadmap and Technical Integration
Development of a unified SKU combining search and shopping agents is underway for a July rollout. Technical teams are prioritizing infrastructure requirements for seamless retailer integration.


Scaling Support and Resources
A custom tool was deployed to scale technical support and provide automated FAQ responses. This initiative aims to reduce query volume directed at product management teams.


Next steps
                                       * Share slides: Distribute the presentation deck to all meeting participants for their review.
                                       * Send pricing metrics: Compile metrics comparing Constructor costs against current product pricing and email the results to Robbie.
                                       * Send pricing feedback: Document and report specific cases where potential business was lost because of current variable pricing structures to the product team.
                                       * Schedule follow-up: Organize a meeting in 2 weeks to review the detailed roadmap vision.
                                       * Share Keynote: Share the link to the Darian keynote video with the team.
                                       * Provide Token Service Steps: Send the steps for setting up the token service for customers.
                                       * Approve Slides: Obtain formal approval for the presented slides for external use.
                                       * Distribute Checklist: Retrieve and distribute the readiness checklist created by the North American AI sales specialists.
                                       * Create Support Channel: Create or facilitate a chat channel for the team to ask technical questions.
                                       * Share Roadmap: Notify the team once the final roadmap is approved.
Details
                                       * Meeting Introduction and Current Pipeline Status: Jonny Burgess welcomed the team and Gretchen Kingry to discuss the GECX shopping agent, noting interest in the retail space. Jonny Burgess highlighted that Bunnings and Kmart Australia are currently live, with more retailers in the pipeline. They established that the meeting would focus on product basics, pricing, integration, and readiness prerequisites to prepare the team for future engagements.
                                       * Shopping Agent Background and Success Metrics: Gretchen Kingry introduced themself as the OPM for the shopping agent, noting their background from the Big Sir AI acquisition and their head PM Venode Ramachandran’s experience. Gretchen Kingry emphasized that Bunnings has been the standout customer, achieving a 5.7% interaction rate within three weeks compared to the typical 1-2% baseline. They argued that this success is primarily due to Bunnings educating their employees and consumers on how to use the agent through marketing videos.
                                       * Product Features and Multimodality: Gretchen Kingry detailed advanced shopping agent features, specifically highlighting the virtual try-on capability for apparel and home remodels. This feature allows consumers to upload images of themselves or their spaces. They noted that these capabilities, supported by Nano Banana, are significant differentiators, though consumer education remains critical for users to understand that image uploads are an option alongside voice and text inputs.
                                       * Data Privacy and Ownership: Gretchen Kingry addressed concerns regarding data security, asserting that Google does not take or use any customer purchase data for the Google Shopping graph. They described the product as a "power strip" that integrates Google tools onto a retailer’s site while the brand retains full ownership of their customer data, journey, and brand voice.
                                       * Consumer Experience and Universal Checkout: Gretchen Kingry shared a personal example of using the agent for e-bike accessories to illustrate how the tool creates a frictionless, efficient shopping experience that saves time. They also mentioned Google I/O updates regarding universal checkout within a chat interface, arguing that retailers should embrace this "agentic" shift rather than fearing that consumers will leave their sites.
                                       * Product Description Page (PDP) Injection Points: Gretchen Kingry discussed the upcoming launch of PDP injection points, which will place dialogue boxes beneath the "add to cart" button. These boxes will provide personalized prompts based on session navigation. Gretchen Kingry stated that this feature is expected to increase interaction rates to 5% or higher, based on previous experience at Big Sir AI.
                                       * Retailer Caution and Market Strategy: Jonny Burgess and Gretchen Kingry discussed why some brands, such as Macy's, Home Depot, and Ulta, adopt a "tiptoe" approach by limiting agent placement or avoiding aggressive marketing. Gretchen Kingry reiterated that Bunnings succeeded because they went beyond standard PR and actively educated their consumer base, which remained the key driver for high interaction.
                                       * AI Commerce Search Integration: Gretchen Kingry noted that the team is developing a new integration process aimed at allowing customers with existing AI commerce search to connect to the shopping agent within a single day. The roadmap for this process is currently under leadership review, with a goal to have the first test running by Friday.
                                       * Financial Value and Cost Efficiency: Michael Scutt raised the potential for using the "free" search integration as a competitive differentiator against third-party providers like Constructor. The participants discussed the need to present a strong ROI business case, acknowledging that current pricing is a hurdle for customers. Gretchen Kingry confirmed that leadership is currently overhauling the pricing strategy to address these budget challenges.
                                       * Roadmap and SKU Consolidation: Gretchen Kingry and Jonny Burgess discussed the roadmap for combining the AI commerce search and shopping agent into a single SKU. They asked the team for feedback on whether customers would prefer this bundle, as they evaluate the potential sales benefits before the next follow-up session in two weeks.
                                       * AI Overview Launch: Gretchen Kingry announced that AI Overview, which allows users to interact with an agent directly from the search bar, is planned for a July rollout. This feature will be available exclusively to customers who have implemented both the shopping agent and AI commerce search, creating a unified experience for long-form queries.
                                       * Agent Capabilities and Future Roadmap: Gretchen Kingry outlined upcoming features including agent-to-agent interoperability, deep personalization via account login history, and bidirectional voice. They clarified that the voice feature was deprioritized due to low engagement during previous implementations, while live avatars are being developed in collaboration with DeepMind.
                                       * Avatar Utility Discussion: Jonny Burgess questioned the ROI and potential "gimmicky" nature of the avatar feature. Gretchen Kingry explained that while it may not fit every brand, retailers like QVC have expressed interest. Andrew McCrohan noted that they had seen high-quality, realistic avatar demos for Lowe's, suggesting the technology is more advanced than cartoonish interpretations.
                                       * Agent-to-Agent Interoperability and Business Insights: Gretchen Kingry explained that interoperability would allow agents to share backend information to improve business decisions. They provided an example where a security camera company used conversation data to discover that customers were using cameras for pet monitoring, which allowed them to launch a successful marketing campaign and increase sales.
                                       * Universal Commerce Protocol (UCP) and Checkout: Gretchen Kingry and Nicholas Diamond discussed UCP, which will eventually enable checkout directly within the chat. They clarified that this uses the retailer's existing payment systems and does not involve Google collecting PII, which addresses data privacy concerns often raised by retailers.
                                       * Data Security and Anomaly Detection: Amy Romano asked about the agent's ability to filter "garbage" or incorrect images during interactions. Gretchen Kingry explained that for virtual try-ons, many merchants limit inputs, but for returns or general inquiries, the system leverages CX Agent Studio to set parameters and guardrails rather than managing complex returns directly within the shopping agent.
                                       * Implementation and Readiness Requirements: Gretchen Kingry emphasized that the primary requirement for standing up the agent is the customer's token service. They noted that while high-bandwidth APIs are beneficial, they are "nice to haves" rather than strict requirements for initial deployment. Gretchen Kingry promised to share a readiness checklist used by North American teams with the group.
                                       * Support Channels and Scaling: The team discussed the need to organize support communication to avoid overwhelming Gretchen Kingry. They agreed to create a technical channel for the region, similar to one used by AI specialists, and emphasized using the provided NotebookLM tool as a primary resource for FAQs before escalating questions to the team.
                                       * NotebookLM Demonstration: Han Wen Kam concluded the meeting by demonstrating a custom NotebookLM instance designed to help the team scale. This tool allows users to ask questions, view generated maps of implementation timelines, and access frequently asked questions. This resource is intended to provide quick answers for salespeople, with Han Wen Kam planning to keep it updated with new information as it becomes available.
                                       * Deployment of Tool for Asia-Pacific Cloud Full-Time Employees: Han Wen Kam shared a link to a new tool via the chat function with Asia-Pacific cloud Full-Time Employees and requested that they reach out if the link is inaccessible. Han Wen Kam noted that the tool is not currently available elsewhere in the Asia-Pacific region and cautioned that users should expect some errors due to associated caveats.
                                       * Field Support Strategy for Product General Availability: Gretchen Kingry encouraged team members to direct their questions about the tool to them, noting that the Operations Program Manager team is fielding the majority of inquiries from the field. This approach is intended to limit the impact on Product Managers as they focus on achieving General Availability for the product.
                                       * Meeting Conclusion and Roadmap Update: Jonny Burgess thanked Gretchen Kingry for their participation in the Tuesday evening meeting, and Gretchen Kingry committed to contacting the group once they have obtained the final roadmap.

[Optional] Office Hours - New AI GTM Tech Operating Model


Description: These optional Office Hours are an opportunity for our non-AI GTM community to ask questions about the new operating model with the introduction of the AI GTM Org.

Meeting notes:


Notes:


Summary
Deployment statuses were reviewed and comprehensive launch checklists, capacity planning, and rigorous testing protocols were established.


Deployment and Launch Readiness
Teams confirmed Tunnelm rollout status and reviewed standardized launch checklists. These guidelines help prevent production regressions and quota breaches.


System Capacity and Probers
System capacity must account for demand spikes and fan-out behaviors. Probers serve as defense mechanisms and should achieve over 90 percent success rates before launch.


Testing and Observability Standards
Teams must perform load testing for traffic projections and maintain 80 percent unit test coverage. Quality evaluations and red teaming are required to ensure agent compliance.


Next steps
                                       * [ ] [Sanghmitra Johri] Check Deployment: Verify the status of the tunnelm deployment request and inform Yogesh of the findings.
                                       * [ ] [Sana Ansari] Review Documentation: Read the documentation regarding local access to Vertex AI for implementation testing.
                                       * [ ] [The group] Create Launch Report: Generate a launch report for new merchants by following the defined checklist parameters and referencing the provided sample.


Details
                                       * Tunnelm Deployment Status: Sanghmitra Johri inquired about the status of a deployment request for Tunnelm that had not been addressed. Yogesh Kumar confirmed that the broad rollouts and Continuous Deployment (CD) push were completed the previous night, and Sanghmitra Johri agreed to cross-check this information and provide an update.
                                       * Customer Launch Checklist Context: Yogesh Kumar provided an overview of the launch checklist required for onboarding new customers, which aims to prevent regressions, quota breaches, and incorrect production changes. They introduced a documentation guide and a sample Kmart launch report to serve as a reference for necessary parameters and to provide context for on-call support teams.
                                       * Capacity Planning Requirements: Yogesh Kumar outlined the capacity planning process to ensure system health for components such as the Commerce Backend, model endpoints, Vertex AI Search, the Harpoon client, and Spanner. They explained that calculations must account for behaviors like request fan-outs, where a single user request might trigger multiple downstream product detail fetches, and suggested applying scaling factors for demand spikes and interaction rates.
                                       * Quota Verification for Services: Yogesh Kumar detailed the process for verifying quotas, noting that Commerce Backend endpoints, including those for configuration retrieval, session initialization, and chat execution, require specific Queries Per Second (QPS) limit checks. They explained that if model quotas reach their limits, users can place new orders or consult the model quality team. Additionally, teams must review quotas for Vertex AI Search and Spanner, specifically checking database cell consumption and disk usage, to ensure sufficient headroom exists before a launch.
                                       * Prober Implementation and Strategy: Yogesh Kumar defined probers as a defense mechanism intended to stop rollouts if a product fails, recommending that they be enabled with the is_production flag set to true one week before launch. Probers should be established in the designated directory to cover all Critical User Journeys (CUJ), such as product discovery and add-to-cart flows. For 3rd-party API integrations, teams may need to secure non-expiring tokens or configure additional API calls to retrieve fresh tokens to avoid blocking the probers.
                                       * Vertex Integration and Prober Testing: Shivank Awasthi, Sana Ansari, and Yogesh Kumar discussed the challenges of token management and Vertex configuration. Yogesh Kumar explained that for Vertex integration, enabling configuration and providing a P4SA service account allows the prober service account to automatically access Vertex endpoints. They also clarified that running probers locally involves specific constraints regarding P4SA identity that require approvals to resolve.
                                       * Prober Monitoring and Success Rates: Yogesh Kumar emphasized the importance of monitoring prober dashboards to assess success rates, with a goal of keeping failure rates between 2% and 3%. Probers should be implemented several days before going live, aiming for a success rate greater than 90% over the final days. Any failures should be investigated, and troubleshooting guides should be included in the launch report to assist on-call staff if issues arise.
                                       * Monitoring and Alerting Configuration: Yogesh Kumar advised on verifying monitoring and alerting setups, referencing sample Change Lists (CLs) that enable conversation insights. They highlighted internal dashboards for tracking the commerce agent, where teams can view error ratios per client ID, and the Pantheon link, which provides visibility into external end-user experiences such as QPS, error rates, and latency. The specified Service Level Objective (SLO) for service availability is 99.9%.
                                       * Load Testing Protocols: Yogesh Kumar detailed that load testing should be performed before a launch to verify that the service can handle projected traffic, such as 100 QPS. Teams can utilize provided Bash scripts for this testing and should document their capacity analysis, monitoring data, and any errors encountered during the test to confirm the service is ready for production.
                                       * Rollout Procedures and Unit Test Coverage: Yogesh Kumar explained that main server rollouts generally require 13 to 14 hours, while Continuous Deployment (CD) pushes for configuration files occur daily between 9:30 AM and 5:00 PM Pacific Time. They stated that unit test coverage should ideally reach 80%, with 100% coverage for all critical user journeys, and these metrics are accessible via Code Search.
                                       * Quality Evaluation and Red Teaming: Yogesh Kumar noted that teams must configure evaluation datasets with specific scenarios to test agent quality, such as ensuring the agent adheres to policies regarding medical or legal advice. They recommended using the Hacker Agent for red teaming and stress testing, which allows developers to simulate different personas to attempt jailbreaks or unauthorized interactions, with results reported through integrated, automated processes.
                                       * Final Launch Documentation and Observability: Yogesh Kumar discussed documenting latency metrics, such as the 5-second P95 target, and stressed the importance of version validation to prevent merchants from using unintended testing versions. They also addressed observability integration, which requires a bug report for the Product Manager to enable, and recommended maintaining separate production and development agents for future feature work.
