Biweekly gTech Study Hall: 10-Minute Weekly Lightning Talk


Description:
Introducing gTech Study Hall: A 2-part Learning & Development session held every Friday during our team’s dedicated ‘Learning Time’ block
                                       * Skills-centric lightning talk [11-11:10AM PST every other Friday]
                                       * "Think of this like a quick, infotainment podcast that does the heavy research lifting for you and then archives 'episodes' to a shared team folder for anytime-access."
                                       * Session topics posted in advance, topic requests welcomed, and tl;dr takeaways included. 2019-2020 Topic Calendar | Submit Topic Requests | go/gTechStudyHall Digital Learning Database
Attachments: gTech Study Hall - Learning Database


Ateendees:


Meeting notes:


Notes:
The following are key takeaways extracted from the provided gTech Study Hall - Learning Database:
                                       * Neuroplasticity: The frontal lobe is the brain's command center for decision-making and planning, and it is the first to decline at age 40. Brain plasticity allows neurons that fire together to wire together, which is the mechanism behind memory creation. Sleep (8-9 hours) and exercise are critical for stimulating the hippocampus and facilitating cell growth.
                                       * Machine Learning: Unlike traditional programming, machine learning allows systems to learn from data to recognize patterns and objects without being explicitly programmed. The process involves identifying features, recognizing patterns, and adjusting weights based on error rates.
                                       * Augmented Reality: Unlike Virtual Reality, which shuts out the real world, AR superimposes digital content onto a live camera feed. Computer vision (geometry and semantics) is essential for mapping AR content into the 3D world correctly.
                                       * Trust: Trust is built in small "Sliding Doors" moments. In an organization, building trust involves the "Anatomy of Trust" (Boundaries, Reliability, Accountability, Vault, Integrity, Non-judgment, and Generosity). Leaders can sabotage trust through fear and dominance, which inhibit the release of oxytocin, a neurochemical essential for cooperation.
                                       * Positive Psychology: According to research, the ratio of positive to negative comments in a leadership team or relationship is a significant determinant of success/health. High-performing teams typically exhibit a ratio of roughly 5 positive comments for every 1 negative.
                                       * Presentation Skills: The most critical parts of a presentation are the beginning and the end. Audiences remember the start and end best, while the middle is often least remembered. Presenters should aim to distill their message into 15 words or less and focus on the problem before the solution.
                                       * Dunning-Kruger Effect: This cognitive bias explains why low performers often overestimate their abilities, as they lack the knowledge required to realize they are incompetent. Conversely, top performers often underrate themselves. The best way to combat this is through continuous learning and exposure to new skills.
                                       * Google Maps: Technology has evolved from a digital atlas to a predictive tool powered by machine learning and user-contributed data. Its future involves more contextualized, predictive modeling (e.g., forecasting natural disasters) and AR integration, though this depends heavily on user willingness to share data and interact within the platform.

The following key points have been extracted from recent Capacity Office Hours documentation:
                                       * Regional Capacity: Reports have been made regarding stockouts in northamerica-northeast1 and m1-ultramem-160 availability issues.
                                       * VM Migrations and Families: GCP is no longer ordering legacy hardware, specifically VM families including N1, E2, N2, N2D, C2D, and C2. Customers are advised to collaborate on migrations to newer VM families.
                                       * Specific Region Constraints: There have been observed stockout scenarios for c4d_highmem in europe-west3-b and m3 in europe-west3-a, as well as N2D capacity constraints in eu-southwest1 and us-central1.
                                       * Best Practices: It is recommended that customers report stockouts if issues persist. Regional planners review and update bugs regarding capacity assessments, and users are encouraged to utilize resources like go/ccat for availability information and go/capacity360 for usage history.
                                       * Machine Family Guidance: Newer VM families (such as Gen4) are recommended for growth, as some older generation capacity is finite and not growing.

Meeting notes:
                                       * Cross-Cloud Architecture: The discussion focused on cross-cloud agent mesh integration and migration strategies for Agent Development Kit (ADK) platforms. The architecture requires identity translation for secure communication between Azure and Google Cloud Platform (GCP) environments, utilizing an API Gateway to manage cross-cloud service requests.
                                       * Governance & AI Gateway: Centralized agent tool governance is a requirement for the target architecture. The team reviewed the AI Gateway pattern provided by Apigee, which facilitates dynamic routing, token rate limiting, and the application of consistent model armor templates across different models and clouds.
                                       * Security & Connectivity: Private connectivity can be established between GCP and Azure, allowing services to remain unexposed to the public internet while using private IP addresses and multi-region availability.
                                       * Platform Roadmap: Google Cloud is pivoting to an agentic-first approach, introducing features such as agent sandboxes, agent memory banks, and enhanced evaluation services.
                                       * Next Steps:
                                       * Share ADK 2.0 documentation with the client.

The Iconic Virtual Try On Overview


Description: Hi Melissa,

Attachments: The Iconic Virtual Try On Overview


Attendees:
melissa.zinman@theiconic.com.au
mu.wang@theiconic.com.au
ivan.bozic@theiconic.com.au
theresa.payne@theiconic.com.au
amyrom@google.com
dfernandezcao@google.com
pcorreia@google.com
xiang.kong@theiconic.com.au


Meeting notes:


Notes:
The meeting focused on the technical integration and business value of virtual try-on technology to improve shopping experiences and reduce return rates.


Summary
                                       * The technology aims to reduce returns by 20% to 50% by addressing previous limitations regarding garment fit and image quality.
                                       * The system includes synthetic identification watermarking and allows clients to set image retention policies to ensure privacy.
                                       * Implementation discussions centered on balancing API latency for mobile users, processing costs, and validating model accuracy for complex garments.
Decisions
                                       * The virtual try-on user experience has a maximum acceptable latency of 10 seconds.
                                       * The rollout strategy will focus on high-value and loyal customers to optimize return rates and manage costs.
Next Steps
                                       * Dan Fernandez Cao will verify regional availability in Australia and the United States, provide non-NDA client examples, research ROI success stories, consult with the Kmart team on mobile optimization, investigate sample terms of service, and organize a model testing workshop.
                                       * The group will conduct testing with non-flat-lay images and coordinate on performance metrics and internal workflow documentation.
Implementation Details
                                       * Integration with existing fit tools, such as FitFinder and True Fit, has demonstrated significant reductions in returns.
                                       * Technical features include a "model carousel" to build user trust, API accessibility via Google Cloud Model Garden, and robust safety guardrails for content moderation.
                                       * Participants discussed metadata management for complex garment types and the necessity of transparent labeling for AI-generated content.
